# Overwrite / full-refresh machinery (#492) — structured map

A note up front that matters for your #518 work: **none of the overwrite lifecycle methods currently take any scope/keys argument** — `begin_overwrite`/`commit_overwrite`/`abort_overwrite` are all `async fn(&amp;self)`. A SCOPED/WINDOWED variant will need either new args threaded through these three methods (and the `Pipeline::run` call sites) or a new `WriteMode` variant + a scope field on `WriteSpec`. Also note a **code/docs drift**: `.claude/rules/{connectors,architecture}.md` say "ES excluded, 6 sinks", but `OVERWRITE_SINK_KINDS` in `cli/src/registry.rs` now lists **elasticsearch** too (7 kinds, via #494 alias-swap). Trust the code.

---

## 1. `crates/core/src/write_mode.rs`

**`WriteMode` enum** — `write_mode.rs:14-32`
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, Serialize, Deserialize, schemars::JsonSchema, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum WriteMode { #[default] Append, Upsert, Delete, Overwrite }
```
`#[non_exhaustive]` is deliberate (comment at :9-13 says adding a variant is an additive minor release — relevant if you add a `ScopedOverwrite`/`WindowedOverwrite` variant). `as_str()` at :34-44 (`Overwrite =&gt; "overwrite"`).

**`DeleteMarker`** — `write_mode.rs:48-54` (`field: String`, `values: Vec&lt;String&gt;`).

**`WriteSpec` struct** — `write_mode.rs:59-72`
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, Serialize, Deserialize, schemars::JsonSchema)]
pub struct WriteSpec {
    #[serde(default)] pub write_mode: WriteMode,
    #[serde(default)] pub key: Vec&lt;String&gt;,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub delete_marker: Option&lt;DeleteMarker&gt;,
}
```
The `#[serde(flatten)]` is applied at the **sink-config** level, not here (see §8). Doc comment at :56-58 explains it's flattened so `write_mode`/`key`/`delete_marker` appear at sink-config top level.

**`WriteSpec` methods** — `write_mode.rs:74-101`
- `validate(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;(), FaucetError&gt;` (:76-84) — errors if `Upsert|Delete` with empty `key`. **Overwrite passes with no key** (test at :448-462 confirms).
- `dedups_by_key(&amp;self) -&gt; bool` (:90-92) — `Upsert|Delete` &amp;&amp; non-empty key.
- `is_overwrite(&amp;self) -&gt; bool` (:98-100) — `matches!(self.write_mode, WriteMode::Overwrite)`. Canonical impl behind `Sink::is_overwrite`.

**`plan_writes(page: &amp;[Value], spec: &amp;WriteSpec) -&gt; WritePlan`** — `write_mode.rs:129-176`. `debug_assert!` at :130-133 that only `Upsert|Delete` reach it — **`Append` and `Overwrite` are routed separately** (the overwrite arm at :151 maps to `false`/upsert but is never reached). `WritePlan` struct at :110-118 (`upserts`, `deletes`, `failed`), `KeyTuple` at :104-105. Helpers `key_to_doc_id` (:230), `key_to_filter` (:243).

**Overwrite tests** — `write_mode.rs:447-469`: `overwrite_mode_flags_and_needs_no_key`, `overwrite_deserializes_from_wire`.

---

## 2. `crates/core/src/traits.rs` — `Sink` trait

All five overwrite/mode methods take **no scope/keys args** (only `&amp;self`); defaults shown:

- `supported_write_modes(&amp;self) -&gt; &amp;'static [WriteMode]` — `traits.rs:466-468`, default `&amp;[WriteMode::Append]`.
- `is_overwrite(&amp;self) -&gt; bool` — `traits.rs:571-573`, default `false`. Doc (:562-570) documents the lifecycle it drives.
- `begin_overwrite(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;(), FaucetError&gt;` — `traits.rs:587-592`, default = typed "does not support write_mode: overwrite" `FaucetError::Sink`. Called once before first `write_batch`.
- `commit_overwrite(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;(), FaucetError&gt;` — `traits.rs:604-609`, default = same typed error. Called once, only after fully successful + uncancelled run.
- `abort_overwrite(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;(), FaucetError&gt;` — `traits.rs:617-619`, default = **no-op `Ok(())`** (best-effort).

Related cleanup-side (#478) methods on the same trait:
- `supports_cleanup(&amp;self) -&gt; bool` — `traits.rs:507-509`, default `false`.
- `cleanup_scope(&amp;self, scope: &amp;BTreeMap&lt;String, Value&gt;, seen: &amp;crate::cleanup::SeenKeys) -&gt; Result&lt;u64, FaucetError&gt;` — `traits.rs:524-534`. **This is the read-side sibling that DOES take a scope** — the natural model to mirror for your windowed variant. `scope` is AND-ed equality predicates in destination-column terms; returns rows deleted.

Default trait test: `default_overwrite_methods_reject_or_noop` — `traits.rs:754-764`.

---

## 3. `crates/core/src/pipeline.rs` — lifecycle driver

`Pipeline::with_cleanup(policy: Arc&lt;CleanupPolicy&gt;)` — `pipeline.rs:286-289`.

**Overwrite lifecycle inside `Pipeline::run`'s streaming path:**
- Columnar fast-path is disabled when overwriting — `pipeline.rs:451-454` (`&amp;&amp; !wrapped_sink.is_overwrite()`).
- `let overwriting = wrapped_sink.is_overwrite();` — `pipeline.rs:535`
- `if overwriting { wrapped_sink.begin_overwrite().await?; }` — `pipeline.rs:536-538` (before the page loop; `?` aborts the run if begin fails).
- The page loop runs via `run_stream` (:540-541) or, with cleanup, wrapped by `CleanupTracker` (:542-593).
- **Finalize** — `pipeline.rs:596-613`:
```rust
if overwriting {
    let cancelled = self.cancel.as_ref().is_some_and(|c| c.is_cancelled());
    match &amp;run_result {
        Ok(_) if !cancelled =&gt; wrapped_sink.commit_overwrite().await?,
        _ =&gt; { if let Err(e) = wrapped_sink.abort_overwrite().await { tracing::warn!(...) } }
    }
}
```
Success+uncancelled → commit (propagates commit errors via `?`). Any error OR cancel → abort (best-effort; abort failure is logged, not propagated — :604-610).

Note the cleanup delete (#478) fires at :540-593 (after `run_stream` returns Ok, only when not cancelled) via `CleanupTracker::new(&amp;wrapped_sink, &amp;policy)` (:549), then `tracker.finish(&amp;policy)` (:563). Cleanup and overwrite are mutually exclusive by config (expand gate, §7) but structurally both live in this same finalize region.

Lifecycle tests — `pipeline.rs:2231-2372`: `overwrite_lifecycle_begins_then_commits_on_success` (:2284), `..._aborts_on_write_failure` (:2297), `..._aborts_on_cancel` (:2315), `non_overwrite_sink_skips_the_lifecycle` (:2332), `overwrite_abort_failure_is_logged_not_propagated` (:2347). Mock `RecordingSink` at :2231-2280 records call order.

`CleanupTracker` signatures (`crates/core/src/cleanup.rs`): `CleanupPolicy` struct :83, `SeenKeys` :142, `CleanupTracker&lt;'a, S: Sink + ?Sized&gt;` :237; `new(inner: &amp;'a S, policy: &amp;CleanupPolicy)` :245, `finish(&amp;self, policy: &amp;CleanupPolicy) -&gt; Result&lt;u64, FaucetError&gt;` :262, `tracked(&amp;self) -&gt; usize` :282; `impl Sink for CleanupTracker` :288.

## 4. Postgres sink — `crates/sink/postgres/src/sink.rs`

Imports `use faucet_core::util::quote_ident;` (:7). Quoting helpers: `quote_ident` lives in **`crates/core/src/util.rs:21-23`** = `format!("\"{}\"", name.replace('"', "\"\""))`. `qualified_table_ref(schema: Option&lt;&amp;str&gt;, table: &amp;str) -&gt; String` — `sink.rs:59-64` (`"schema"."table"` or bare quoted).

**Staging naming** — `sink.rs:198-214`:
- `staging_table_name(&amp;self) -&gt; String` (:200-202) = `format!("{}__faucet_ovw", self.config.table_name)`.
- `effective_table_name(&amp;self) -&gt; String` (:208-214) — returns staging name when `self.config.write.is_overwrite()`, else the configured table. **This is how `write_batch` transparently routes writes into staging during an overwrite run** (used at :1067-1068, insert path).

**Trait impls** — `sink.rs:793-885`:
- `supported_write_modes` (:794-801) → `[Append, Upsert, Delete, Overwrite]`.
- `is_overwrite` (:803-805) → `self.config.write.is_overwrite()`.
- `begin_overwrite` (:812-839): `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {staging}` then `CREATE TABLE {staging} (LIKE {target} INCLUDING DEFAULTS)`. Uses a single acquired conn. Target must already exist (error message hints at :834).
- `commit_overwrite` (:845-868): opens `tx = pool.begin()`, runs three statements in order `TRUNCATE TABLE {target}` / `INSERT INTO {target} SELECT * FROM {staging}` / `DROP TABLE {staging}`, then `tx.commit()`. Postgres runs TRUNCATE+DDL transactionally so the swap is atomic.
- `abort_overwrite` (:872-885): `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {staging}` (best-effort).

Construction guardrails in `PostgresSink::new` — `sink.rs:160-196`: COPY write-method is allowed with overwrite (comment :173-175) but rejected for upsert/delete (:176-187). `write_batch` insert path (:1067-1109) uses `effective_table_name` implicitly; `write_batch_partial` (:1120-1131) treats append+overwrite as insert-shaped (no per-row key failures).

---

## 5. BigQuery sink — `crates/sink/bigquery/src/sink.rs` + `idempotent.rs`

Table-ref helper: `idempotent::table_ref(project, dataset, table) -&gt; String` = `` format!("`{project}.{dataset}.{table}`") `` (`idempotent.rs:219-221`). Sink's own `table_ref(&amp;self)` at `sink.rs:307-313`.

**Staging naming** — `sink.rs:315-327`:
- `overwrite_temp_id(&amp;self)` (:316-318) = `format!("{}__faucet_ovw", self.config.table_id)`.
- `overwrite_temp_ref(&amp;self)` (:321-327) → backtick-quoted via `idempotent::table_ref`.

**Bucket-free page load** — `insert_overwrite_page(&amp;self, records: &amp;[Value]) -&gt; Result&lt;usize, FaucetError&gt;` — `sink.rs:334-359`. Serializes the page to a JSON `@payload` string, builds SQL via `idempotent::build_insert_select(&amp;columns, project, dataset, &amp;self.overwrite_temp_id())` (:339-344), runs `jobs.query` with NAMED param mode (deliberately NOT streaming `insertAll`, comment :329-333). Routed from `write_batch` at `sink.rs:759-763` and :817-819 when `self.config.write.is_overwrite()`.

**Trait impls** — `sink.rs:887-936`:
- `supported_write_modes` (:885-891) → `[..., Delete, Overwrite]`.
- `is_overwrite` (:893-895) → `self.config.write.is_overwrite()`.
- `begin_overwrite` (:902-909): `run_ddl(format!("CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE {temp} LIKE {target}"))` — copies partitioning/clustering, bucket-free.
- `commit_overwrite` (:918-926): `run_ddl(idempotent::build_overwrite_commit_sql(&amp;self.table_ref(), &amp;temp))` then `run_ddl("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {temp}")`.
- `abort_overwrite` (:930-936): `run_ddl("DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {temp}")`.

**SQL generator** — `build_overwrite_commit_sql(target_ref: &amp;str, temp_ref: &amp;str) -&gt; String` — `idempotent.rs:269-276`:
```rust
"BEGIN TRANSACTION;\n\
 TRUNCATE TABLE {target_ref};\n\
 INSERT INTO {target_ref} SELECT * FROM {temp_ref};\n\
 COMMIT TRANSACTION;"
```
`build_insert_select` at `idempotent.rs:279`. `run_ddl(&amp;self, sql: String)` at `sink.rs:364+`. Tests for the SQL builder at `idempotent.rs:611-657`.

## 6. `cli/src/registry.rs`

- `OVERWRITE_SINK_KINDS: &amp;[&amp;str]` — `registry.rs:878-888` = `["sqlite", "postgres", "mysql", "mssql", "mongodb", "bigquery", "elasticsearch"]` (7 — ES included via #494, comment :885-887). Subset relationship to `UPSERT_SINK_KINDS` noted at :876-877.

- `sink_supported_write_modes(kind: &amp;str) -&gt; &amp;'static [WriteMode]` — `registry.rs:964-980`: matches on `(UPSERT_SINK_KINDS.contains, OVERWRITE_SINK_KINDS.contains)` → four arms: `(true,true)` = `[Append,Upsert,Delete,Overwrite]`, `(true,false)` = `[Append,Upsert,Delete]`, `(false,true)` = `[Append,Overwrite]`, `(false,false)` = `[Append]`.

Parity tests: `sink_supported_write_modes_allowlist` (:2038-2065), plus `cli/tests/registry_capability_parity.rs` (referenced :875) asserts allowlist ↔ each sink's `supported_write_modes()` agree.

## 7. `cli/src/expand.rs` — gates

**write_mode × sink validation** — `expand.rs:785-838`:
- Parse `write_mode` string → `WriteMode` (:788-804); unknown → `CliError::Config` "unknown write_mode … (expected append, upsert, delete, or overwrite)".
- `if !sink_supported_write_modes(&amp;merged_sink.kind).contains(&amp;mode)` (:805-821): error names allowlist — for Overwrite it prints `OVERWRITE_SINK_KINDS.join(", ")` ("overwrite sinks: …"), else UPSERT list.
- upsert/delete require non-empty `key` (:822-838).

**Overwrite incompatibility gates** — `expand.rs:849-868` (comment header :840-848 explains ordering-before-delivery-derivation):
```rust
if matches!(mode, WriteMode::Overwrite) {
    if delivery == DeliveryMode::ExactlyOnce { return Err(... "incompatible with delivery: exactly_once ...") }   // :850-856
    if let Some(ref sd) = cfg.pipeline.schema
        &amp;&amp; SchemaDriftPolicy::compile(sd).on_drift == OnDrift::Evolve {
        return Err(... "incompatible with schema.on_drift: evolve ...")                                            // :857-867
    }
}
```
Scoped-cleanup incompatibility is enforced indirectly: the cleanup gate (`expand.rs:975-1030`) requires `write_mode: upsert` (error :1012), so overwrite can never also carry cleanup. Overwrite gate tests: `overwrite_passes_on_capable_sink` (:3061), `rejects_overwrite_on_unsupported_sink` (:3076), `rejects_overwrite_with_exactly_once` (:3095), `rejects_overwrite_with_schema_evolve` (:3114).

Every capable sink follows this pattern (the seven WriteSpec sinks). Both sinks reach the spec via `self.config.write.is_overwrite()`.

## 10. Docs-sync surfaces (per `.claude/rules/maintenance.md`)

The overwrite trigger row is **`.claude/rules/maintenance.md:58`** — it enumerates the exact surfaces to update when changing overwrite support. They are:

**Task: Implement #528 (staged bulk load) — delivered a tested shared foundation + accurate capability flag; ClickHouse/MSSQL execution deferred with rationale.**

**Branch/commit:** worktree `worktree-agent-ae8b3c78f38cf4070`, commit `428324f1` (on top of #527's `a8e923dd`... note: my worktree branched from `209aab69`/main, so it does NOT contain your #527 commit — cherry-pick or merge accordingly).

**Files changed:**
- `crates/core/src/staging.rs` (new, 748 lines) — the shared module.
- `crates/core/src/lib.rs` — module + re-exports (`StagedFile, StagingCleanup, StagingCompression, StagingFormat, StagingLocation, StagingScheme, StagingSpec, serialize_records`).
- `crates/core/src/traits.rs` — new defaulted `Sink::supports_staged_load()`.
- `crates/core/Cargo.toml` — `staging` feature (`= ["dep:object_store", "compression"]`) + optional `object_store` dep (features `aws,gcp,azure`). `Cargo.lock` +1.
- `crates/sink/{redshift,snowflake,bigquery}/src/sink.rs` — override `supports_staged_load()-&gt;true`.
- `cli/src/registry.rs` — `STAGED_LOAD_SINK_KINDS` + `sink_supports_staged_load()` + a unit test.

**Key finding that shaped the decisions:** Redshift (`write_strategy: copy` → `COPY FROM s3://`), Snowflake (`bulk_load` → `COPY INTO @stage`), and BigQuery (`bulk_load` → GCS Parquet load job) **already implement staged bulk load** with their own idiomatic configs. The genuine *gaps* are **ClickHouse** and **MSSQL**.

**Per-sink status:**
- redshift / snowflake / bigquery — **already native**; now advertise `supports_staged_load()` + in the registry allowlist. No behavior change.
- clickhouse — **NOT wired (gap).** Design: `staging` config + pure generator `INSERT INTO t SELECT * FROM s3('&lt;https-url&gt;','&lt;JSONEachRow|CSVWithNames|Parquet&gt;'[,creds])` (scheme→function: s3/gcs/azureBlobStorage), staged branch = core `StageUploader` upload → run INSERT → cleanup.
- mssql — **NOT wired (gap).** Synapse `COPY INTO target FROM '&lt;adls-url&gt;' WITH (FILE_TYPE=…, CREDENTIAL=…)` / `BULK INSERT` via an EXTERNAL DATA SOURCE.
- **Why deferred:** ClickHouse's `s3()` URL form (needs `https://…`, not `s3://`) + credential passing, and MSSQL's external-data-source/SAS setup, are fiddly and **untestable in CI** (need live warehouses); wiring them blind risks the exact silent-correctness bugs the quality bar forbids. I chose to ship a rock-solid, fully-tested foundation + accurate capability flag over sprawling half-verified COPY glue. Recommend finishing ClickHouse+MSSQL in a focused follow-up with LocalStack/live testing.

**Integration notes for you (important):**
- **No `expand.rs` gate needed** for this slice — I did **not** flatten `StagingSpec` onto any sink config; the 3 capable sinks validate their own pre-existing native staging configs. (When ClickHouse/MSSQL get the shared `staging:` field, the gate rule would be: `staging` allowed only when `sink_supports_staged_load(kind)`, and call `StagingSpec::validate(allowed_schemes, allowed_formats)` per sink.)
- **No `schemas/faucet.schema.json` splice needed** — no new sink-config fields were added.
- READMEs: I did **not** add sections (the 3 sinks already document their native staging; deprioritized under a disk/budget constraint) — worth a follow-up line each.

**Tests:** 12 core `staging::` tests (10 pure + 2 uploader via `object_store::memory::InMemory`) + 1 registry test — all pass. `cargo semver-checks -p faucet-core` → **no semver update required** (all additive: new types + a defaulted trait method). `cargo fmt` clean. (Note: a pre-existing clippy `-D warnings` failure in `stage.rs`/`transforming_source.rs` — `variable does not need to be mutable` ×2, `OnePageSource never constructed` — is unrelated to my change; it's in the current tree already.)

**Heads-up:** the shared `target/` hit ENOSPC mid-run; I removed `target/release` + `target/debug/incremental` (regenerable) to free ~11GB — your next build will recompile those.</result>

## Report — 6 payroll/HR/ATS taps → faucet templates

All six written to `drivetrain/templates/&lt;tap&gt;.yaml`, translated from real tap code, following the ramp.yaml pattern. Every one validates under **both** `--param sink=jsonl` and `--param sink=bigquery` (independently re-verified, not just self-reported).

| Tap | Streams | Source | Auth | jsonl | bigquery | Manual work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **adp** | 15 (7 root, 8 child) | rest | OAuth2 client_credentials **+ mutual TLS** | ok | ok | **#511 mTLS (blocking for real run)** |
| **isolved** | 2 (1 root, 1 child) | rest | OAuth2 client_credentials | ok | ok | minor #511 note |
| **paylocity** | 2 (1 root, 1 child) | rest | OAuth2 client_credentials | ok | ok | none |
| **rippling** | 1 | rest | OAuth2 **refresh-token** → token_endpoint | ok | ok | **#511 flow-auth (blocking for real run)** |
| **lever** | 14 (7 root, 7 child) | rest | HTTP Basic (api_key) | ok | ok | none |
| **navan** | 2 | rest | OAuth2 client_credentials | ok | ok | runtime fan-out gap (below) |

### Auth translations (faithful to source code)
- **adp**: `oauth2` (token_url `/auth/oauth/v2/token`, scope `read`) + client cert on every request. `tls:` block left commented with `# TODO(#511)` — tap ships `cert_public`/`cert_private` as base64-encoded PEM that it decodes to files at runtime; faucet needs raw PEM + the rest `mtls` feature. Must be re-enabled for production.
- **rippling**: refresh-token grant mapped to `token_endpoint` best-effort. Real flow can't be fully expressed — it POSTs form-urlencoded with client creds in an HTTP Basic header (not JSON body) and **rotates the refresh_token on every refresh** (persisted to disk by the tap). Detailed `# TODO(#511)` left inline.
- **isolved**: `oauth2` client_credentials; token endpoint uses `client_secret_basic` (Basic header) — noted as a possible #511 nuance if faucet's oauth2 provider doesn't send creds that way.
- **lever**: Basic auth, api_key as username / empty password (Lever convention).
- **paylocity / navan**: standard `oauth2` client_credentials, real token_urls and scopes.

### Per-parent child fan-out (translated as matrix `parent:` + `${parent.field}` tokens)
- **adp**: workers → 6 children via `${workers.associate_oid}`; job_requisition → questionnaire; payroll_output → payroll_output_acc.
- **lever**: opportunities → 7 children (application, offer, attachment, form, referral, panel, resumes) via `${opportunities.id}`.
- **isolved**: client → employee via `${client.id}`. **paylocity**: employee_list → employee via `${employee_list.employee_id}`.

### Non-#511 caveats worth flagging
- **navan `transaction` stream**: the tap fans a single table across 9 transaction types via a query-param loop, modeled here as `partitions`. The streaming path (`stream_pages`) does not honor `config.partitions` (only `fetch_all` does), so a single `overwrite` run won't fan out across all 9 types — needs a custom fan-out or per-type append+dedup. Also has derived `trip_*` columns and a 2-month rolling window (no relative-month `${now.*}` token, so `date.from` is a param defaulting to `2020-01-01`).
- **adp payroll_output_acc**: the tap's `get_child_context` is commented out (inert in Meltano today); modeled as the code intends — verify against ADP before enabling.
- **lever**: `OpportunityInterviewsStream` is defined but unregistered in the tap, so intentionally omitted.

Shaping in every file mirrors z3z1ma: per-stream `json_encode` of the tap's real `convert_to_string` fields + `keys_case snake` (plus `flatten` where the tap flattens). Sinks are exactly ramp.yaml's (`write_mode: overwrite`, `application_default`). Nothing outside `drivetrain/` was touched and no git was run.</result>

## Taps needing manual / follow-up work

- **dualentry** — 9 of 52 streams are parent→child fan-outs the tap does imperatively and are **not emitted** (documented in the file header): 7 line-item streams (`GET {parent}/{number}/lines/` per parent record) and 2 path-parameterized child streams (`contract_usage_records`, `fixed_asset_depreciation_schedules` — the latter a fixed-assets × depreciation-books cross-product). Also `journal_entries` here fetches the slim list only; the tap re-hydrates its `items` from a date-windowed `/journal-entry-lines/` sweep. These need faucet matrix parent/child wiring.
- **campfire** — `budget_accounts` and `subscriptions` are parent-scoped paths (`{budget_id}`/`{contract_id}`) whose ids the tap first lists from another endpoint; emitted as single rows with unfilled placeholders (validate-only) + TODO to wire as matrix parent/child. `chart_transactions`/`journal_entries` use monthly date windows in the tap — collapsed to a single full-range window (same row union; noted for optional `window:` conversion).
- **afas** — streams are fully dynamic (one per AFAS GetConnector); template is parameterized on a single `connector` (run/clone per connector). The `AfasToken` header expects the token pre-Base64-encoded — the tap's raw-hex/raw-XML→base64 encoding isn't reproduced (TODO in header). Dynamic base URL handled via `${param.environment_id}`.
- **invoiced** — z3z1ma target used `upsert:true`; template uses `write_mode: overwrite` per the ramp pattern (note in header on switching to `upsert` + `key:[id]`). Tap's epoch→ISO date-field rewrite in post_process is not reproduced (noted).
- **greenhouse** — incremental floor sent as `&lt;rk&gt;=gte|&lt;RFC3339&gt;` via `replication_bind` (tap uses `.000Z` millis; API accepts both).

No tap required #511 flow-auth, async-job, or SOAP work. General note across brex/afas/greenhouse: those taps JSON-stringify nested fields either via `flatten_keys`/`expand[]` (brex) or by auto-stringifying *all* nested values (afas, greenhouse) — faucet's `json_encode` needs explicit field lists, so I followed the ramp.yaml recipe and flagged the divergence inline where the existing BigQuery table may expect flattened/stringified columns.</result>

## Taps needing manual / #511 / follow-up work (flagged in-file)

- **ms365bc** — biggest gap. Real tap does **multi-company fan-out with a dynamic per-company base URL** (`GET /companies` → one URL per company, rows tagged `company_id`/`company_name`, optionally across multiple environments). faucet has no dynamic base-URL enumeration, so the template targets **one company per run** (company_id/company_name params, annotations reproduced via a `set` transform) — needs flow-auth (#511) or run-once-per-company + UNION. Big line-item `$expand` arrays are left as nested arrays (as the tap emits); only `dimensionSetLines` is JSON-stringified.
- **restaurant365** — the 12 high-volume views (transaction, transaction_detail, sales_*, payroll_summary + the 5 filtered Transaction streams) are fetched by the tap in **sliding 30-day date windows** (R365's &gt;31-day `$filter` cap + server SQL timeouts on large `$skip`). faucet's `odata:` block does plain `@odata.nextLink` paging with no windowing, so a full backfill of those may 400/500 — needs windowed paging before production. Master-data streams page cleanly.
- **shopify** — the two **ShopifyQL** streams (sessions, sales) are **omitted**: they use offset pagination over columnar `tableData.rows` (arrays, not node objects) with a composite key — not expressible with faucet's cursor/graphql extraction; needs custom work. Also a documented **flatten divergence**: the tap flattens `flatten_keys` one level (`parent_child` + JSON-encode deeper), whereas faucet `flatten` recurses fully — verify column names before flipping to bigquery.
- **ms365fno** — works as-is via OAuth2 v2 `scope` (functionally equivalent to the tap's v1 `resource=` flow, noted in-file); the tap's alternative `refresh_token` grant would need `token_endpoint` auth. Dynamic `entities_to_sync` → parameterized single entity per run.
- **netsuite_pro** — dynamic `reports` (`report_id:report_name`) → parameterized single report per run (run once per report).
- **redshift** — dynamic `tables` list → parameterized single table per run; native discrete `host/port/user/credentials/query` fields (no `connection_url`).

One note on shaping: `keys_case snake` is applied on every template per the z3z1ma convention in the rules, even where the tap itself doesn't snake-case (ms365fno passes rows through) — worth confirming against an existing table at flip time for that one.</result>

Positions streams are metadata+humanReadable driven with cursor-in-body paging.

Dynamic `reports`/`v1_reports` streams are tenant-config-driven (UUID:name) so not statically enumerable — documented as add-one-matrix-row-per-report, with the v1 date-pivot explode flagged custom.

All six templates written to `drivetrain/templates/&lt;tap&gt;.yaml` and validate `ok:` under `--param sink=jsonl` **and** `--param sink=bigquery`. All follow ramp.yaml exactly: `default` source template, dual `jsonl`/`bigquery` sinks (bigquery `write_mode: overwrite`, `application_default` auth), one matrix row per stream, per-row `json_encode`(convert_to_string) → `keys_case: snake` shaping, and `sink: { ref: "${param.sink}" }`.

| Tap | Streams (rows) | Source | Auth | jsonl | bigquery |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **quickbooks** | 35 (27 entity + 8 report) | rest (QBO query API + `/reports`) | OAuth2 refresh via `flow` (Intuit token endpoint needs HTTP **Basic** — via `basic_auth` param) | ok | ok |
| **xero** | 20 (14 entity + 4 projects + 2 report) | rest (2 base URLs: api.xro + projects.xro) | OAuth2 refresh via `flow` (Bearer + `Xero-tenant-id` header) | ok | ok |
| **zohobooks** | 27 (20 entity + 7 report) | rest (region-templated URL) | OAuth2 refresh via `flow` (`Zoho-oauthtoken` prefix) | ok | ok |
| **rillet** | 18 (13 entity + 5 report) | rest (Bearer; `v3` variant adds `X-Rillet-API-Version`) | static Bearer / custom-headers | ok | ok |
| **attio** | 15 (5 root + 10 child) | rest (GET + POST-query) | Bearer(api_key) | ok | ok |
| **deel** | 4 (2 root + 2 child) | rest | Bearer(auth_token) | ok | ok |

**Reshape handling:**
- `tree_flatten` on the report Rows/Columns trees: QuickBooks (8 report streams, `$.Rows.Row` + `ColData`/`Columns.Column`), Xero (2 reports, `Rows`/`Cells`), Rillet (3 statement reports).
- `unpivot` (wide→long): Deel `gp_gross_to_net` (pay-component map), Attio `record_value` + `entries_value` (attribute value-maps). Attio `list_workspace_member_access` uses `explode`.
- **Zohobooks reports were translated exactly to the tap's real behavior — it emits section rows and `json_encode`s the nested subtree columns rather than tree-flattening** (downstream dbt parses the JSON); the two `accounttransaction` report streams dive to leaf rows via a 2-level `records_path`. I chose fidelity to the code over the generic tree_flatten heuristic and noted it in the file header.
- Rillet report/invoice streams use a `v3` custom-headers source variant (static token, so a Bearer-carrying custom header works). Deel/Zoho/Xero/Attio use matrix **parent→child fan-out** (nested chains validate, incl. Deel's 3-level legal_entities→reports→gross_to_net).

**Items flagged for manual/#511 or runtime follow-up (all documented as in-file TODO comments; all still validate):**
- **QuickBooks** — Intuit token endpoint requires Basic auth; `flow` has no base64-of-static helper, so `basic_auth` (base64 of client_id:client_secret) is a supplied param. QBO query-API pagination (`STARTPOSITION`/`MAXRESULTS` embedded *inside* the SQL `query` string) isn't injectable, so entity streams pull one 1000-row page. Report adaptive date-windowing / cell-splitting / BQ pre-DELETE are procedural (not reproduced); `columns=` (mandatory for full GL columns) omitted.
- **Xero** — flow sends client creds in the token-request form body; Xero officially prefers Basic on the token step (noted). `journals` cursor-by-`JournalNumber` offset and per-stream `If-Modified-Since` incremental header aren't expressible → first-page / full-pull.
- **Zoho** — region `ca` uses a different accounts host (`accounts.zohocloud.ca`); synthetic `gl_accounts` + `account_transactions` GL fan-out streams (per-account fan-out + client SHA-256 `gl_line_key` + saturation paginator) are **omitted** as non-declarative. Report date-window params left as TODO.
- **Deel / Attio** — matrix fan-out into a shared table under `write_mode: overwrite` is per-invocation (last-writer-wins across parents); parent-context-derived columns (e.g. Deel `legal_entity_id`), Attio's `id`-object spread, POST-body offset paging, double-explode of value-maps, and conditional grandchild fetches are noted as declarative-model gaps.

## Report

**Tap name:** `tap-onedrive` — actually the DrivetrainAi fork of **`tap-sharepointsites`** (variant `storebrand`) driven with `mode: onedrive`. Confirmed from `onedrive/meltano.yml`: single extractor `tap-sharepointsites`, config `mode: onedrive` + `refresh_token`/`client_secret`.

**OAuth2 refresh confirmed (not app-only):** `client.py` `authenticator` — `mode=="onedrive"` POSTs `grant_type=refresh_token` to `https://login.microsoftonline.com/common/oauth2/v2.0/token` with `client_id`+`client_secret`+`refresh_token`. The `sharepoint` mode uses `ClientSecretCredential` (app-only); that path is unused here. Base URL grounded in `FilesStream.url_base = https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0`; OneDrive download path `_resolve_download_url → /me/drive/items/{id}/content`, where `{id}` = pre.py `idAtSource` (= tap `file_pattern`).

**Matrix rows mapped** (onedrive has **no fixed streams** — pre.py generates one `files:` entry per tenant file/sheet from an S3 config; rows are one-per-file examples to clone):
- `headcount_csv` → CSV drive item (`response_format: csv`, `csv_delimiter ","` = tap CSVHandler default)
- `budget_xlsx` → Excel sheet, wide (`response_format: excel`, `excel_sheet`)
- `revenue_timeseries` → Excel `data_format_type: "timeseries"` (#516) → inbuilt `unpivot` to `(dimensions, month, value)`

Style matched to brex/rest_file exactly: `version: 1`, `name: tap-onedrive`, params (`sink` default bigquery, required secrets, `bq_project`=REPLACE_ME_PROJECT, `bq_dataset`=raw), `pipeline.sources.default` (rest + `response_format`), pipeline `keys_case: snake` (= tap `normalize_name`/`clean_colnames`), `jsonl`+`bigquery` sinks (`write_mode: overwrite`, `auth: application_default`).

**Faucet gaps found:**
1. **`oauth2_refresh` sends no `scope`.** The tap sends `scope=https://graph.microsoft.com/.default offline_access`; faucet's `OAuth2RefreshProvider::from_config` reads only `token_url`/`client_id`/`client_secret`/`refresh_token`/`expiry_ratio` and omits `scope` on the grant. Azure AD reuses previously-consented scopes so tokens still issue for Graph, but strict-scope tenants could break.
2. **Refresh-token rotation persistence not on by default.** Microsoft rotates the refresh token on every use; faucet only persists the rotated token if the provider is given a durable `persist:`/`state`. As shipped (config-seed only), a second run would fail once Azure invalidates the seed. Documented in the template's auth block with a `persist:` example; fine for one-shot migration.

One faithful omission noted in-file: the tap stamps `_loaded_at` (utcnow) per row — audit-only, left out.

Both gaps and the dynamic-stream nature are documented in the source-of-truth top comment. File written and validated via `yaml.safe_load_all` (1 doc, no errors).</result>

## Summary

**Tap name:** `tap-oracle-fusion` (repo `DrivetrainAi/tap-oracle-fusion`, wired in `meltano_taps/oracle_fusion/meltano.yml`).

**Matrix row ids mapped (1 report):**
- `trial_balance_mis_report` → `streams.py` `TrialBalanceMISReport`, report_path `/Custom/Financials/General Ledger/TRIAL BALANCE MIS REPORT/Consolidated TB Input File - MIS Report.xdo`. The tap ships exactly one report stream (`ALL_STREAMS = [TrialBalanceMISReport]`), so one matrix row.

**Grounded in real code:**
- SOAP 1.1 `runReport` against `{base_url}/xmlpserver/services/v2/ReportService`, `SOAPAction: runReport`, `text/xml` — reproduced faithfully via the xml source `soap:` block, including the exact `&lt;v2:runReport&gt;/&lt;v2:reportRequest&gt;` envelope with `flattenXML=true`, `sizeOfDataChunkDownload=-1`, and the 5 params from `_params_for_period` (P_LEDGER_NAME, P_PERIOD_TYPE=YTD, P_PERIOD_NAME=`%b-%y`, PHP_RATE, INR_RATE), plus `&lt;v2:userID&gt;/&lt;v2:password&gt;` embedded in the body as the tap does.
- Auth = Basic (username/password), also mirrored into the SOAP body per `base.py`.
- Response = base64 XLSX inside `&lt;reportBytes&gt;`, headers on row 4 (0-indexed) — documented and shown in the intended decode chain.
- Target overwrite:true/denormalized (z3z1ma) → `write_mode: overwrite`, `auth: application_default`.
- Matched brex.yaml exactly: `version:1`, `name: tap-oracle-fusion`, params block (sink default bigquery, required secret `password`, `bq_project`=REPLACE_ME_PROJECT, `bq_dataset`=raw), `pipeline.transforms` keys_case snake, `pipeline.sinks.jsonl` + `.bigquery`, matrix rows.

**Faucet gap (template is NOT yet end-to-end runnable — documented in a prominent `# LIMITATION:` block):**
Consuming Oracle Fusion needs BOTH a raw `text/xml` SOAP POST AND the #515 base64→xlsx decode, but no single source does both:
- The **xml** source posts the SOAP body (soap: block) but has **no `decode:` pipeline** — it only navigates XML elements, so it yields the base64 `&lt;reportBytes&gt;` blob as one string and can't base64-decode/parse the embedded XLSX.
- The **rest** source **has** the `decode:` pipeline (base64 + `parse: xlsx` with `header_row`) but **can't POST raw text/xml** — `body` is a JSON `Value` sent via `req.json()` (always application/json, confirmed at `crates/source/rest/src/stream.rs:1434`), and its decode `extract` step parses the body as JSON (`decode.rs` `serde_json::from_slice`), so it can't pull `&lt;reportBytes&gt;` out of a SOAP envelope.
- **Fix needed (either):** add the #515 decode pipeline to the xml source with an XML-aware `extract` step; OR give the rest source a verbatim text/xml body mode + an XML `extract` decode step. The intended decode chain is included commented on the row.
- Secondary gap: the tap's monthly period fan-out (start_date→now, one `runReport` per `%b-%y` month) has no faucet equivalent for SOAP-body fan-out; the row runs one period via `${param.period}` (defaults to current month).

**Validation:** `python3 yaml.safe_load_all` parses cleanly (1 document); gh auth restored to `pawan-dt`.</result>

## Results

**Tap name:** `tap-sharepointsites` (DrivetrainAi fork, `storebrand` variant) — SharePoint files fetched over Microsoft Graph.

**Grounded in real code** (`DrivetrainAi/tap-sharepointsites` + `meltano_taps/sharepointsites`):
- **Auth** (`client.py`): mode `sharepoint` = Azure AD **app-only client_credentials** via `ClientSecretCredential(tenant_id, client_id, client_secret).get_token("https://graph.microsoft.com/.default")`. Reproduced with faucet's inline `oauth2` provider against `login.microsoftonline.com/&lt;tenant&gt;/oauth2/v2.0/token`, scope `graph/.default` — the exact shape ms365bc.yaml uses.
- **Download** (`file_stream.py`): the tap does a 3-hop resolve (site_url → default drive → item → `/content`). faucet has no multi-hop, so I used Graph's equivalent **single-request path form** `GET /v1.0/sites/{host}:/{type}/{name}:/drive/items/{item_id}/content` (item_id = the tap's `file_pattern` = `idAtSource`), addressing the same default document-library drive.
- **Parsing**: CSVHandler/ExcelHandler → `response_format: csv|excel`, `excel_sheet` (= `sheet_name`), `csv_delimiter`, pagination `None` (file = one body).
- **Timeseries** (`excel_handler.py::_timeseries_rows`): `data_format_type: timeseries` unpivots date-header period columns to long `dims + Month + Value`. Reproduced with the inbuilt **`unpivot`** transform (#516): `id_fields` = dimension columns, periods collapse into `month`/`value` (default `columns` = all non-id).
- **Shaping**: `normalize_name`/`clean_colnames`/target snakecase → `keys_case snake`. Target `overwrite:true` → `write_mode: overwrite`, BQ `auth: application_default`.

**Matrix rows mapped** (3 REPLACE_ME exemplars of the three real file shapes pre.py fans out; duplicate per tenant file list):
- `headcount_csv` — CSV file (wide/transaction)
- `budget_actuals_xlsx` — Excel sheet, wide (transaction)
- `opex_forecast_timeseries` — Excel sheet, timeseries → `keys_case` + `unpivot`

Style matches rest_file.yaml/ms365bc.yaml exactly (`version: 1`, `name`, secret params, `sink` default bigquery, `bq_project`/`bq_dataset`, `sources.default` rest, `sinks.jsonl`+`sinks.bigquery`).

**Faucet gaps noted in the top comment:**
1. **No dynamic drive resolution** — path-form targets only the site's *default* library; a file in a non-default library needs flow-auth/multi-hop (#511).
2. **Excel needs a build with the rest `excel` feature.**
3. **unpivot period key** — the tap emits `Month` as a parsed DATE (header cell `.date()`); faucet `unpivot` puts the raw header text into `month`. Added an optional `cast … timestamp` follow-up (faucet has no pure DATE cast type — only integer/float/bool/string/timestamp).

Validated: `python3 yaml.safe_load_all` passes (1 doc). gh auth restored to `pawan-dt`.</result>

## Result

**Tap name:** `tap-skyslope` (repo `DrivetrainAi/tap-skyslope`, wired in `DrivetrainAi/meltano_taps/skyslope/meltano.yml`).

**File:** `/Users/c-sikawats/Documents/GitHub/Personal/faucet-stream/faucet-local-demo/drivetrain/templates/skyslope.yaml`

**Grounded in real code:** `base_url = https://api.skyslope.com/api` (base.py `url_base`); records paths per stream (`$.value.contacts[*]`, `$.value.users[*]`, `$.value.offices[*]`, `$.value[*]`, `$.value.listing`, `$.value.sale`); bulk date-window (`type=listing|sale` + `modifiedAfter/modifiedBefore` / `createdAfter/createdBefore`, bulk.py yearly `DateRangePaginator`); `convert_to_string` lists and `flatten+snake` shaping (base.py `standardize_data`); index→GUID N+1 (base.py `TapSkyslopeDetailStream`, `links[rel=next]` paging).

**Matrix rows mapped (10 rows for 8 registered streams):**
- `contacts`, `users`, `offices` — top-level (no paging)
- `listings`, `sales` — bulk `/files` date-window streams
- `summary` — `/files?type=summary`
- `listings_index` (parent) → `listings_detail` (child) — N+1, `${listings_index.listing_guid}`
- `sales_index` (parent) → `sales_detail` (child) — N+1, `${sales_index.sale_guid}`

The two `*_index` rows are auxiliary enumeration parents that mirror the tap's internal index-then-detail-by-GUID mechanism (they write to jsonl scratch tables; the child rows write the real `*_detail` tables). Matches brex/lever style exactly: `version:1`, `name: tap-skyslope`, params with `sink` default bigquery + secret credentials + `bq_project`/`bq_dataset`, `sources.default` rest boilerplate, `keys_case snake` + `json_encode` transforms, jsonl + bigquery(overwrite, application_default) sinks.

**faucet gaps (documented as `LIMITATION:` in the top comment):**
1. **Request signing (the big one).** SkySlope needs (a) a per-request HMAC-SHA256 signature over `client_id:client_secret:{fresh-utc-timestamp}` (key = `secret_key`, base64) placed in `Authorization: SS {access_key}:{sig}` + a matching `Timestamp` header, and (b) a prior `POST /auth/login` (itself HMAC-signed) yielding a `Session` token that must ride every request as a `Session` header. The rest source's `Auth` enum (None/Bearer/Basic/ApiKey/ApiKeyQuery/OAuth2/TokenEndpoint/Custom) has **no** HMAC/request-signing variant, and #511's request-signing flow signs method+path+timestamp+body (a different recipe) and can't do the login→dynamic-header step. Modeled as `auth: type: custom` placeholder headers with the full recipe spelled out.
2. Bulk yearly date-range batching collapsed to a single `[start_date, now.iso]` window.
3. `*_detail` fingerprint-skip incremental state → full fetch each run.
4. `PropertyAddressMixin.property_address` computed column on sales/sales_detail omitted (no computed-column transform).</result>

- all streams `replication_key = None` → `FullTable` (no incremental, so no `replication_bind` needed)

- `revenue` (v3.1, page_size 250, PK [customer_id, timeframe], filter param, flatten_keys customer)
- `contracts`, `invoices`, `payments`, `obligations`, `customers` (PK [id], page_size 500)

- `renewals` (PK [id], no json_encode)
- `arr` (`/reports/arr`, PK [customer_id, timeframe, currency], filter param, flatten_keys customer)

`revenue_filter` maps to the `filter` query param on `revenue` + `arr` only (default `""`, set `currency:eq:INR` for Indian tenants). Dead config `start_month` and the `streams_to_discover` selector intentionally omitted (the matrix enumerates streams).

**Faucet capability gaps flagged in-file:**
1. **flatten_keys** — the tap flattens only the `customer` object into `customer_*` columns; faucet's `flatten` transform is global (all nested objects). Left as a commented opt-in (`- { type: flatten }`) per the brex precedent; `customer` otherwise lands as a nested RECORD.
2. **PageNumber termination** — faucet stops on a zero/repeated page vs. the tap stopping on first short page → one extra trailing request (same data).
3. **Conditional filter param** — the tap omits `filter` when empty; faucet always sends `filter=` (empty string) on revenue/arr. Noted; harmless for USD tenants, required non-empty for INR.

`query_params_multi` and `replication_bind` were not applicable (no repeated array params; no incremental keys in the tap). gh auth is on `pawan-dt` as requested.</result>

**Capability gaps hit (documented in-file with `# LIMITATION:` / `# NOT REPRODUCED`):**
1. **`oauth1` build feature** — CLI must be built `--features oauth1`.
2. **account host vs realm** — the tap derives both the URL host (`replace("_","-").replace("SB","sb")`) and OAuth realm (`upper().replace("-","_")`) from one value; faucet params can't string-transform, so I take two params (`account` + `realm`).
3. **`Prefer: transient` header** — the rest source's `headers` field is `#[serde(skip)]`, so the header the tap always sends can't be set from YAML.
4. **Offset ceiling (~99000)** — the real tap ID-batches + date-windows around NetSuite's SuiteQL offset cap (ENG-82817); plain faucet Offset pagination has no windowing, so the big transaction-grain streams truncate past ~100k rows on large accounts (flagged per-row).
5. **Not reproduced** (no faucet equivalent): dynamic column discovery (used `SELECT *`), DEFAULT_COLUMNS projection, ID-batch sizing, id→date "switch replication", and the per-window BQ cleanup for line streams — replaced by straight full-table `SELECT *` → overwrite.</result>

## faucet gaps (worked around, documented in the top comment as LIMITATIONs)
1. **No OAuth2 `password` grant** in faucet-auth (only client_credentials / oauth2_refresh / token_endpoint / oauth1). Modeled with the shared-catalog `flow` provider (#511): POSTs the password-grant form → captures `$.access_token` → applies `Bearer`. The `flow` provider is also the only way to inject the required `x-Infor-MongooseConfig` header, because the REST source's static `headers` map is `#[serde(skip)]` and unsettable from YAML.
2. **No keyset/filter-advancing pagination.** Modeled with `replication_method: Incremental` + `replication_bind` (#513) rendering the Mongoose `filter` (`format: raw`, extra_filters folded into the template). Consequence: one `recordCap` page per run, bookmark walks forward across runs; the tap's parallel intra-run keyset batching (PARALLEL_WORKERS/BATCH_SIZE/strides) is not reproduced.
3. **Int-keyset lexical-bookmark caveat:** SyteLine returns `TransNum` as a string and faucet reads the replication key from the raw (pre-transform) record, so cross-run advancement compares it lexically — reliable only when lexical == numeric order. A `cast` transform is included but doesn't change source-side bookmarking.

2. **No `resultId`/`readMore` continuation** — faucet's xml source can't read `resultId` from the response body and rewrite the POST body into a `readMore` envelope. **As written, only the first `pagesize` page is fetched per object**; large objects (GLDETAIL) are truncated until faucet gains body-cursor→body-injection paging.
3. **No matrix-row-id string interpolation** — since faucet only interpolates `${param.*}`/`${now.*}`, the per-object `&lt;object&gt;` tag can't be templated centrally, so each matrix row overrides the full `body` (control/login stay DRY via params).
4. **xml source field names best-effort** — `body`/`headers`/`records_path`/`pagination` weren't verified against this build's schema; flagged to run `faucet schema source xml` if `validate` rejects them.</result>

**Two faucet gaps flagged in-file:**
1. **`#511` cookie-session login** — real auth is POST `/entity/auth/login` → `Set-Cookie: ASP.NET_SessionId` (204, empty body, so `token_endpoint` can't even read it) with 401→re-login. faucet has no flow-auth, so modeled as `type: custom` sending a `Cookie` header from an out-of-band `${param.cookie}`.
2. **`#511b` windowed replication** — the tap's `WindowedReplicationPaginator` tiles history into calendar-month `$filter` windows to dodge Acumatica's non-strict `$orderby` skip-gap bug (ENG-77809). faucet's Offset paginator has no month-window generator, so the template does a single unbounded `$top/$skip` pass (safe for the single-page static dims; skip-gap risk noted for large document tables).

The `journal_transaction_detail` parent-cache child fan-out (tap stashes the parent's `$expand=Details` array in an in-process cache keyed by BatchNbr+Module) has no faucet equivalent, so it's re-modeled as re-requesting `/JournalTransaction` with `$expand=Details` and an `explode` transform on `Details` (1→N) — documented inline.</result>

<summary>Agent "Implement auth-flow #541 #542" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Both enhancements are implemented in `crates/auth/src/flow.rs` only. All 75 tests pass (also with `--features oauth1`), clippy is clean with `-D warnings`, and formatting is clean. No dependencies added. Changes left in the working tree, uncommitted.

## What changed

**#541 — HMAC `sign` on login/pre-flight steps.** Added `sign: Option&lt;SignSpec&gt;` to `FlowRequest` (reuses the existing `SignSpec`). In `run_login`, after the request body is built, the signature is computed from a fresh `${ts}`/`${nonce}` clock merged with the captured context so far, then attached as a header. Extracted a shared `sign_header(&amp;SignSpec, &amp;ctx) -&gt; (name, value)` helper now used by both `run_login` and `build_request_auth`'s `apply` signer. Validation rejects an empty `sign.into.header` on a step. `deny_unknown_fields` preserved.

**#542 — capture from more than JSON bodies.** `FlowStep.capture` is now `HashMap&lt;String, CaptureSpec&gt;`, an **untagged** enum: a bare string stays `from: json` (back-compat), or a `{ from, name|path }` struct selects `header` / `set_cookie` / `xml`. `run_login` reads headers before consuming the body and only reads/parses the body when a json/xml capture needs it. Hand-rolled a tiny in-crate XML tokenizer + tree walker (`xml_dot_path`) — handles the XML declaration, attributes, self-closing tags, CDATA, the 5 predefined entities, and namespace-prefix stripping (`ns:tag` matches `tag`); no dep on the xml source crate. Set-Cookie selection by cookie name, case-insensitive header lookup. The shared-cookie-jar variant is out of scope, noted as future work in a doc comment on `FlowConfig`.

### New config shapes

Signed login step (SkySlope):
```yaml
steps:
  - request:
      method: POST
      url: "${param.base_url}/auth/login"
      json: { clientId: "${param.client_id}" }
      sign:
        alg: hmac_sha256
        key: "${param.secret_key}"
        template: "${param.client_id}:${param.client_secret}:${ts}"
        encoding: base64
        into: { header: "Authorization", format: "SS ${param.access_key}:${sig}" }
    capture: { session: "$.session.token" }
```

Capture sources (Acumatica Set-Cookie / Intacct XML):
```yaml
capture:
  session_cookie: { from: set_cookie, name: "ASP.NET_SessionId" }
  sess_id:        { from: xml, path: "response.operation.result.data.api.sessionid" }
  loc:            { from: header, name: "Location" }
# existing bare-string form still valid: { access_token: "$.access_token" }
```

### Tests added (in `flow.rs`)
- `sign_header_computes_name_and_value_with_clock`
- `signed_login_step_sends_computed_signature_header` (asserts the exact computed header via wiremock header matcher)
- `skyslope_style_signed_login_then_signed_data_requests` (end-to-end: signed login → capture → signed+session data request)
- `config_rejects_empty_step_sign_header`
- `xml_dot_path_extracts_nested_text`, `xml_dot_path_ignores_declaration_attrs_and_namespaces`, `xml_dot_path_handles_cdata_and_entities`
- `set_cookie_and_header_helpers_select_by_name`
- `capture_backcompat_string_form_parses_as_json` (proves old form → `from: json`)
- `capture_struct_forms_parse_kinds`
- `config_rejects_capture_missing_selector`
- `capture_from_header_works`, `capture_from_set_cookie_applies_as_cookie`, `capture_from_xml_body_works`, `capture_from_xml_miss_is_an_error`

**Dependencies added:** none.

**README:** updated the `flow` section (field table, a "Signing a login step" subsection, and a "Capture sources" subsection with the source table + future-work note on the cookie jar).

Files touched: `crates/auth/src/flow.rs`, `crates/auth/README.md`.</result>

<result>All clean — only intended references remain. Both enhancements are complete.

## Summary

Implemented both enhancements in `crates/source/rest` only. No commits/pushes made. **No dependencies added.**

### #539 — settable custom request headers

- `crates/source/rest/src/config.rs`: `headers` changed from `#[serde(skip, default)] pub headers: HeaderMap` to a serde/JsonSchema-friendly `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if=...)] #[schemars(with="std::collections::HashMap&lt;String,String&gt;")] pub headers: HashMap&lt;String,String&gt;`. Added a `build_header_map()` helper that converts the string map into a validated `HeaderMap`, mapping invalid names/values to typed `FaucetError::Config`; it's called from `validate()` (fail at load) and reused at request time. Updated the `header()` builder, the `Default`, and the OData `Prefer` default-insertion (now case-insensitive) to the string map.
- `crates/source/rest/src/stream.rs`: `RestStream` gained a `static_headers: HeaderMap` built once in `new()` (after `apply_odata_defaults` + `validate`). All three request paths now start from `static_headers` and apply auth on top, so **an auth header of the same name wins** — the normal per-page request (`execute_request_once`), the async-job requests (`job_request_bytes`), and the OData `$metadata` discovery probe. `${env:}`/`${param.*}` interpolation is already handled at load time on config strings; values ride the existing redaction boundary since headers are ordinary config strings.

YAML shape:
```yaml
source:
  type: rest
  config:
    headers:
      Prefer: transient
      Accept: application/json
      X-Tenant: ${env:TENANT_ID}
```

### #543 — async_job `fetch.url_from`

- `crates/source/rest/src/async_job.rs`: `JobRequest.url` is now `Option&lt;String&gt;` and a new `url_from: Option&lt;String&gt;` field was added. `AsyncJobConfig::validate()` rewritten to require exactly one of `fetch.url`/`fetch.url_from` (blank strings count as unset), reject both/neither, and reject `submit.url_from`.
- `crates/source/rest/src/stream.rs`: `run_async_job` now retains the last (successful) poll body (`let ... = loop { ... break body; }`) and resolves the fetch URL from `url_from` via JSONPath (absolute URL used verbatim, relative resolved against `base_url`; no string match → typed `FaucetError::Source`), falling back to the templated `url`.

YAML shape (Stripe report run):
```yaml
async_job:
  submit: { method: POST, url: "/v1/reporting/report_runs", json: {...} }
  job_id: "$.id"
  poll:   { url: "/v1/reporting/report_runs/${job_id}", status_path: "$.status" }
  status: { path: "$.status", success: [succeeded], failure: [failed] }
  fetch:  { url_from: "$.result.url" }
```

### Tests (all pass)

Unit (`config::tests`): `headers_serde_round_trip_as_string_map`, `validate_rejects_invalid_header_name`, `validate_rejects_invalid_header_value`; updated the odata assertion for the map type.
Unit (`async_job::tests`): `validate_fetch_url_xor_url_from`, `validate_rejects_url_from_on_submit`.
Integration `tests/headers_test.rs` (new): `config_headers_sent_on_every_request` (wiremock header matcher across 2 paginated requests), `auth_provider_header_overrides_config_header` (provider `Authorization` beats config header; non-conflicting config header survives).
Integration `tests/async_job_test.rs`: `async_job_fetch_url_from_poll_body` (submit → poll pending→succeeded with `result.url` → fetch → CSV decode → 2 rows), `async_job_url_from_no_match_errors`.

### Results
- `cargo test -p faucet-source-rest`: **all pass** (133 lib unit tests + all integration binaries + 2 doctests; new tests green).
- `cargo clippy -p faucet-source-rest --all-targets -- -D warnings`: **clean**.

### Docs
Updated `crates/source/rest/README.md`: rewrote the `headers` row + added a "Custom request headers" subsection (precedence, interpolation, validation), and updated the async-job `fetch` row + added a "Resolving the download URL from the poll body (`fetch.url_from`)" subsection with the Stripe example.

Note: `JobRequest.url` became `Option&lt;String&gt;` (public, re-exported type) — a technically breaking signature change confined to this crate; the orchestrator regenerates the committed JSON schema from these structs.</result>

```yaml
auth:

    type: flow

            json: { name: "${param.username}", password: "${param.password}", company: "${param.company}" }

      reauth_on: [401]
```

The login POSTs `{name, password, company}`, captures `ASP.NET_SessionId` from the 204's `Set-Cookie`, replays it as a Cookie on every data request, and auto-re-logs-in on 401.

```yaml
pagination:
  type: BodyCursor
  next_token_path: "response.operation.result.resultId"
  next_body: |
    &lt;request&gt;
      &lt;control&gt;…sender creds…&lt;/control&gt;
      &lt;operation&gt;&lt;authentication&gt;&lt;sessionid&gt;${session_id}&lt;/sessionid&gt;&lt;/authentication&gt;
      &lt;content&gt;&lt;function controlid="q1"&gt;&lt;readMore&gt;&lt;resultId&gt;${next_token}&lt;/resultId&gt;&lt;/readMore&gt;&lt;/function&gt;&lt;/content&gt;&lt;/operation&gt;
    &lt;/request&gt;
```
readMore rides the captured session and loops until `resultId` is absent/empty/repeats. It's generic across all objects (readMore is keyed only on the session-scoped resultId), so the per-object `records_element_path` in each row still applies to the paged responses.

I could not read the tap source (only meltano `.lock` files are present locally; the tap ships from `git+https://github.com/DrivetrainAi/tap-intacct.git`), so the exact `getSession` function name and response paths are set from Sage Intacct's documented XML gateway structure and the paths you supplied — `getAPISession` / `response.operation.result.data.api.sessionid` for capture, `response.operation.result.resultId` for the cursor. Two things to confirm against a live `faucet validate` / `faucet schema source xml` on this build:
- Whether `resultId` is emitted as an element vs a `&lt;data resultId="…"&gt;` attribute after XML→JSON conversion (Intacct returns it as a `&lt;data&gt;` attribute) — the `next_token_path` relies on the source's namespace-insensitive trailing-match dot-path resolving it either way.
- I left the per-object readByQuery bodies self-authenticating with `&lt;login&gt;` (per your "keep bodies intact" instruction). Since Intacct's `resultId` is session-scoped, for readMore continuation to resolve against the same session in production those bodies should ride `${session_id}` (swap their `&lt;authentication&gt;&lt;login&gt;…` for `&lt;authentication&gt;&lt;sessionid&gt;${session_id}&lt;/sessionid&gt;`). ## VERDICT: **Partial — 3 of 5 streams are fully config-driven; 2 (ShopifyQL) are a genuine gap.**

I read the real tap (`DrivetrainAi/tap-shopify`: `tap.py`, `base.py`, `streams.py`, `queries.py`, `constants.py` + the `meltano_taps/shopify/meltano.yml`). **There is NO Bulk Operations API, NO REST, and NO async JSONL export anywhere in this tap** — so `async_job`/`decode`/`jsonl`-parse are not applicable here. Every stream POSTs to one endpoint: `{store_url}/admin/api/2026-01/graphql.json`. Auth is the header `X-Shopify-Access-Token: &lt;api_token&gt;` (custom, not bearer).

### Per-stream mechanism
| Stream | Class | Mechanism | Faucet? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **orders** | `ShopifyStream` | Admin GraphQL top-level `orders` connection: `(first:250, after:$cursor, query:$query, sortKey:UPDATED_AT)`, Relay cursor paging `pageInfo{hasNextPage,endCursor}`, records `edges[*].node`, incremental via `query="updated_at:&gt;=&lt;cutoff&gt;"` | ✅ graphql source |
| **customers** | `ShopifyStream` | same connection shape | ✅ graphql source |
| **products** | `ShopifyStream` | same connection shape | ✅ graphql source |
| **sessions** | `ShopifyQLStream` | `shopifyqlQuery(query:$q)` GraphQL field, **OFFSET pagination** (LIMIT/OFFSET baked into the ShopifyQL string, stop when rows &lt; 250), columnar `tableData.rows` | ❌ gap |
| **sales** | `ShopifyQLStream` | same ShopifyQL/offset mechanism | ❌ gap |

**Critical finding on nested GraphQL connections:** orders' `lineItems`/`shippingLines`/`refunds`/`fulfillments` (and products' `variants`) are fetched **inline with a fixed `first:N` and are NOT independently paginated even by the tap** — it just takes the first N. So faucet's single-top-level-cursor limitation is a non-issue for this tap; the graphql source reproduces the record set exactly. (The known nested-sub-connection paging gap does not bite here.)

### What I updated (config-only, everything achievable)
- Kept orders/customers/products on the **graphql source** and made them **more faithful** than the prior template: added the tap's incremental `query:$query, sortKey:UPDATED_AT` (threaded via a new `start_date` param → `variables.query = "updated_at:&gt;=&lt;start_date&gt;T00:00:00Z"`), matching `build_connection_query` exactly.
- Per-stream `json_encode` (tap's `convert_to_string`) + `flatten` + `keys_case snake`, `write_mode: overwrite` (z3z1ma `upsert:true`+`denormalized`), matrix fan-out one table per stream, jsonl|bigquery param flip.
- sessions/sales documented as an explicit `# LIMITATION`, with the correct single-page request shape left commented (not shipped — a single-page pull would silently truncate on a green run, the worst failure class).

### Genuine gaps + concrete proposed faucet features
1. **Offset-into-variable pagination** (blocks sessions/sales). The offset lives inside a *sub-string* of the GraphQL `q` variable, and termination is "page returned &lt; page_size" — not a `pageInfo` boolean. The graphql source only injects an `endCursor` into an `after` variable; the rest `Offset` paginator only writes an offset into a query-param, not into a nested body string. Proposed config shape:
   ```yaml
   pagination: { type: Offset, offset_variable: q_offset, page_size: 250,
                 stop_when_short: true, template: "... LIMIT 250 OFFSET ${q_offset}" }
   ```
   Crate: `faucet-source-graphql` (reusing the offset logic already in `faucet-source-rest`'s `pagination/`).
2. **Columnar-row zipping** (secondary, sessions/sales). `tableData.rows` are positional arrays needing to be zipped against `tableData.columns[].name`. Proposed: a `zip_columns` transform (crate: `faucet-core` `stage.rs`), or expressible today via the existing `sql`/`wasm` transform.
3. **Minor shaping divergence (not a mechanism gap):** the tap flattens *only* listed keys, *one level*, JSON-encoding nested dict/list (e.g. `totalPriceSet_shopMoney` as a JSON string); faucet's `flatten` recurses fully (`totalPriceSet_shopMoney_amount`). Fine for a fresh overwrite table; noted in the template for anyone appending to an existing tap-created table. A `flatten` option for single-level+selective+json-encode-nested would close it.

## Definitive Report: tap-plaid → faucet-stream

### VERDICT: Fully config-driven for the correct full-refresh reproduction (matches the campfire/brex overwrite pattern). One genuine gap exists — and it only affects true cross-run **incremental** sync, not output correctness.

I read the real code (`DrivetrainAi/tap-plaid` @ `main`: `tap_plaid/base.py`, `streams.py`, `tap.py`, plus the plaid `meltano.yml`). The tap has **exactly two streams**.

**Tap facts (from source):**
- **base_url**: environment-dependent — `https://sandbox.plaid.com` or `https://production.plaid.com` (`PLAID_HOSTS`).
- **method**: `POST` on every endpoint (`http_method = "POST"`).
- **auth**: NOT a header. Credentials go in the **JSON body**: `{client_id, secret, access_token}` (`prepare_request_payload`). Headers are only `Content-Type: application/json` + optional `Plaid-Version` (default `2020-09-14`). `access_token`/`item_id` are two scalar env vars; a connection instance = one Plaid Item.

### Stream-by-stream mechanism used

| Stream | Endpoint | records | Pagination | faucet mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **accounts** | POST `/accounts/get` | `$.accounts[*]` | none | rest POST, body creds, `records_path: $.accounts[*]`, `write_mode: overwrite`. post_process reproduced: `json_encode [verification_insights]` → `flatten` (balances → `balances_*`) → `keys_case snake` → `set {item_id, institution_name}`. ✅ fully expressible |
| **transactions** | POST `/transactions/sync` | `$.added[*]` | cursor: `has_more` + `next_cursor`, cursor re-sent in **body** as `cursor` | rest POST, body `{count:500}`, `pagination: CursorInBody{next_token_path:$.next_cursor, body_cursor_field:cursor}`, `write_mode: overwrite`. Shaping: `json_encode [location,payment_meta,personal_finance_category,counterparties,category]` → `keys_case snake` → `set {item_id,institution_name}`. ✅ full-refresh path fully expressible |

**Cursor mechanics confirmed empirically** (`crates/source/rest/src/pagination/mod.rs`): `CursorInBody` reads `$.next_cursor` and injects it into the request body at `body_cursor_field`, and **stops on null/absent OR a repeated cursor**. Plaid returns a `next_cursor` even on the final page (`has_more=false`); when faucet re-sends it, Plaid returns the *same* cursor + empty `added` → repeat-guard trips → clean termination. So within-run paging is correct today without any `has_more` predicate.

### Resolving the KEY QUESTION (added / modified / removed)

The elegant, correct answer is that **for the demo you do not need upsert+delete rows at all**. On a cursor-less run (which is all faucet can do today — see gap A) Plaid returns `added` = the *complete* current history, with `modified=[]` and `removed=[]`. So `$.added[*]` + `write_mode: overwrite` reproduces the exact end state, and deletions are handled naturally (a source-deleted txn is absent from the next full pull). This is identical to how the campfire/brex templates already work.

The `added→upsert` / `removed→delete` design in the prompt is only meaningful for **true incremental** sync, which is where the real limitation lives.

### The genuine gap (incremental-only) + proposed feature

Two independent reasons faucet cannot reproduce the tap's *incremental* cursor sync config-only:

**Gap A — pagination-cursor persistence.** `CursorInBody` exhausts pages within a run but never saves the terminal `next_cursor` as a resumable bookmark, nor re-seeds it into the next run's first request body. `replication_key`/`replication_bind` only capture a *record field's* max value — Plaid's cursor is an envelope token, not a record field, so it's uncapturable. Result: every run is a full pull (correct, but not incremental).

**Gap B — single-response fan-out to multiple write modes.** Even with A, one `/transactions/sync` response carries `added`+`modified` (→ upsert) *and* `removed` (→ delete) from a **single cursor advance**. faucet's one-`records_path`→one-`write_mode`-per-row model would require two separate passes that each advance and persist the shared cursor independently — they'd diverge, and the removed-delete pass could never see the same delta the upsert pass consumed. A single paged response cannot atomically feed an upsert sink *and* a delete sink config-only.

**Proposed faucet feature (grounded in this tap's behavior):**

*Resumable pagination cursor (closes A).* Add to the rest source pagination config:
```yaml
pagination:
  type: CursorInBody
  next_token_path: "$.next_cursor"
  body_cursor_field: cursor
  persist_cursor: true          # NEW: emit terminal cursor as StreamPage.bookmark
  stop_on: "$.has_more == false"  # NEW (optional): explicit terminator vs repeat-guard
replication_bind: { source: bookmark, target: body, field: cursor }  # NEW: seed next run's first request from the saved cursor
```
Crate: **`faucet-source-rest`** — `pagination/mod.rs` (carry the terminal cursor into `StreamPage.bookmark`) + `stream.rs` (read the stored bookmark and inject it into the first request body/param on resume). No `faucet-core` trait change needed (rides the existing `StateStore` bookmark path).

*Multi-array response fan-out (closes B).* A rest source option to emit multiple labeled record streams from one response envelope, each routed to a different sink write mode from the same page/cursor advance — e.g.
```yaml
records_multi:
  - { path: "$.added[*]",    tag: upsert }
  - { path: "$.modified[*]", tag: upsert }
  - { path: "$.removed[*]",  tag: delete }
```
consumed by a sink/topology that maps `tag → write_mode` in one pass. This is a larger change touching **`faucet-core`** (`StreamPage` gaining tagged sub-batches, or a topology tee that preserves a single cursor advance) plus **`faucet-source-rest`**. Given its size, it is the correct thing to file as a separate `feature`/`tier-2` issue rather than block the demo.

### Deliverable
`faucet-local-demo/drivetrain/templates/plaid-to-bigquery-or-jsonl.yaml` written and YAML-validated (2 streams). It implements everything achievable config-only via the overwrite full-refresh model, exactly matches the campfire/brex shape (params with `sink` default bigquery + secret params + bq_project/bq_dataset, `pipeline.sources.default` rest, jsonl+bigquery sinks with `application_default` auth, one matrix row per stream), and carries one honest `# LIMITATION:` block scoped precisely to incremental sync (gaps A+B) — not to output correctness. No git commit was made.</result>

## VERDICT: Fully config-driven (21/22 streams byte-faithful; 1 stream has 1 non-essential field gap)

The premise that "the Stripe SDK list model isn't byte-identical to a REST pull" is **false**, verified against the code. `stripe.&lt;Object&gt;.list(**params)` is literally `GET /v1/&lt;resource&gt;?&lt;params&gt;`, and `row.to_dict()` returns the **raw** object. The tap uses **no `expand[]` anywhere**, does no auto-expansion of sub-lists, and adds no computed fields at the list level. Every reshape lives in `post_process` (pure, per-record) — all reproducible with faucet transforms. So a raw `api.stripe.com/v1` cursor pull + `json_encode` + `keys_case` reproduces every entity/event stream exactly.

### Per-stream mechanism
- **17 immutable entity streams** (accounts, application_fees, balance_transactions, charges, coupons, credit_notes, customers, disputes, invoice_items, invoices, payment_intents, payouts, plans, promotion_codes, refunds, subscription_schedules, transfers): `rest` Cursor pull on `/v1/&lt;resource&gt;`, `records_path $.data[*]`, `starting_after=$.data[-1].id`. Incremental `created` reproduced with **`window` slicing** (`created[gte]`/`created[lte]`, `format: epoch_s`, `step 30d`). `string_keys`→`json_encode`, snake→`keys_case`. `invoices` pins `Stripe-Version: 2023-10-16` via per-row `headers`.
- **checkout_sessions**: template uses the fuller entity list `/checkout/sessions` (tap uses events; entity list is strictly better for a full snapshot and supports the same `created` window).
- **subscriptions**: entity endpoint with `query_params status=all` + window — faithfully reproduces the tap's forced FULL_TABLE-with-status=all (captures cancelled subs).
- **discounts, subscription_history** (events-only, no top-level list): `/events` + `type` query filter + `records_path: $.data[*].data.object` unwrap — exactly the tap's `sdk_object=Event` + `record_path=["data","object"]`.
- **card_payments_fees_transaction_level_1** (report): fully wired via a dedicated `report` source template — `async_job` submit `POST /reporting/report_runs` (with `report_type` + `parameters[interval_start|end]`) → poll → **`fetch.url_from: "$.result.url"`** (#543, reads the signed `files.stripe.com` link from the poll body) → `decode: csv`. This closes the gap the old template flagged as blocked.
- **invoice_line_items**: omitted to match the tap (disabled in its `discover_streams()`). Not a gap — faucet expresses it as a parent→child matrix fan-out if ever re-enabled.

### The one genuine gap
**`subscription_history.event_id`.** The tap keys this stream on the Event **envelope** `id` (aliased to `event_id`) and keeps every event (dedup disabled). Because `records_path` unwraps to `$.data[*].data.object`, faucet emits the subscription object and **discards the envelope**, so `event_id` can never be recovered by a downstream transform — the field is not in the emitted record. Under this template's `write_mode: overwrite` target every event row is still written (overwrite does no per-key merge), so **no data is lost here**; the gap only bites an upsert-by-key destination that needs a per-event unique key. (The stream's other SDK additions — `customer_id`, `latest_invoice_id`, …, `start` — are renamed *copies* of existing scalars and ARE reproducible via a `type: sql`/duckdb transform; omitted only to keep the template feature-light.)

### Proposed faucet feature (grounded)
**`record_ancestors` — envelope-field lifting for the REST source.** When `records_path` selects a nested array element, allow lifting named fields from the enclosing ancestor object into each emitted record:

```yaml
records_path: "$.data[*].data.object"
record_ancestors:            # relative to the $.data[*] array-element ancestor
  event_id: "id"
  event_created: "created"
```

- **Crate:** `faucet-source-rest`, extract module (`crates/source/rest/src/extract/`) — where `records_path` JSONPath selection already happens; add a post-selection merge that walks each matched leaf's ancestor and copies the named paths onto the record.
- **Why it's the right shape:** it's the exact generalization of the Singer "Events API unwrap + carry envelope id" pattern (Stripe, and any `data.object` webhook/event feed), needs no new transform, and is a small additive config field. It precisely closes `subscription_history.event_id` and makes faucet's events-API story complete.</result>
<usage><subagent_tokens>213075</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>18</tool_uses><duration_ms>501613</duration_ms></usage>

| tap | verdict | detail |

ISSUE — deskera auth (real reproduction failure): tap-deskera `auth.py` POSTs the token endpoint with `Authorization: Basic base64(client_id:client_secret)` and sends the resulting token as the `x-access-token` request header (confirmed in `base.py`). The template's `token_endpoint` provider (a) can only emit `Authorization: Bearer`, and (b) carries no client credentials on the token request at all — so it would fail to mint a token and, even if it did, send the wrong header. Fix: rewrite to the `flow` provider (same pattern as bullhorn) — one step POSTing `token_url?grant_type=refresh_token&amp;refresh_token=…` with a basic-auth sign on the step, `capture: access_token: $.access_token`, then `apply: [{into: header, name: x-access-token, value: ${access_token}}]`. Left unedited because it depends on flow's step-level basic-auth `sign` schema, which I can't verify without inspecting faucet.

GAP 1 (biggest, recurring) — child fan-out into a single full-refresh table is last-writer-wins. matrix parent/child fan-out runs one sink invocation per parent record, and `write_mode: overwrite` commits per-invocation, so each parent overwrites the previous → only the last parent's rows survive. Blocks faithful reproduction of deel (gp_legal_entity_reports, gp_gross_to_net), campfire (budget_accounts, subscriptions), dualentry (9 sub-resource line-item/child streams), deskera (account_subgroups). Needs append-into-prestaged or single-invocation multi-parent enumeration. Crate: `faucet-cli` executor + `faucet-core` overwrite lifecycle (`write_mode.rs` / `Pipeline::run`).

GAP 2 (minor) — `unpivot` keeps each wide cell as one `value` object; deel's tap splits each pay-component cell into label/type/current_value/compare_value/percentage_diff. Crate: `faucet-core` transform (unpivot). Also deskera report streams need calendar-month/day windowing + `%d-%m-%Y` date format + whole-response-as-`report_json` envelope (window step is absolute-duration only, date format is YYYY-MM-DD). Crate: `faucet-core` window/backfill + an envelope transform. Both flagged in-template.</result>

<result>All four files parse cleanly (the `_encode`/`_decode` lines are shell-profile noise, not parser output — every file printed `OK`).

Summary:

- **deskera-to-bigquery-or-jsonl.yaml** → Removed the wrong `token_endpoint` block (which emitted `Authorization: Bearer`) and its stale TODO(#511). Added a top-level `auth: { deskera_flow }` **flow** provider: a login step POSTs the token URL (grant_type + refresh_token in the query string, exactly as the tap), captures `$.access_token`, then `apply` rides it as the `x-access-token` header with `reauth_on: [401]`. Source now uses `auth: { ref: deskera_flow }`. **Note/minor caveat:** the tap authenticates the token request with HTTP Basic `base64(client_id:client_secret)`; faucet's flow `request` has no `basic` field and there's no base64 interpolation helper, so client creds are sent as `client_secret_post` (form body) — the standard OAuth2 equivalent. Documented inline; the load-bearing bug (header placement) is fully fixed. → **validated yes**

- **openai-to-bigquery-or-jsonl.yaml** → Added `query_params_multi: { "group_by[]": [...] }` to `completions_usage` (`project_id, user_id, api_key_id, model, batch, service_tier`) and `costs` (`project_id, line_item`). Rewrote the stale TODO that claimed repeated params were "impossible (query_params is scalar)"; kept the still-real per-bucket envelope-merge gap. → **validated yes**

- **oracle_fusion-to-bigquery-or-jsonl.yaml** → Added a pipeline-level `set` transform (`values: { period: "${param.period}" }`) before `keys_case`, injecting the `period` column the tap sets on every row. The `period` param already existed (default `${now.strftime.%b-%y}`). Comment notes the monthly period fan-out remains a separate limitation. → **validated yes**

- **zohobooks-to-bigquery-or-jsonl.yaml** → Fixed the `ca` host bug. A param `default` can't reference another param (single-pass bind), so I split the accounts domain segment into a new `accounts_domain` param (default `zoho`) and changed the token URL to `accounts.${param.accounts_domain}.${param.region}`. Every region stays auto (`accounts.zoho.&lt;region&gt;`); `ca` becomes correct with one documented override `--param accounts_domain=zohocloud` → `accounts.zohocloud.ca`. Replaced the stale TODO(#511 auth) note. → **validated yes**

No fix required a `# LIMITATION:` bail-out; all four are expressible with existing faucet features. Not committed, per instructions.</result>
